<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.

</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

```
{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
```

jinja can be parsed normally

10 hidden files/directories in this directory are excluded. You can use 'ls -la /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1' to see them.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me first understand the problem clearly:

### 1.1 Problem Analysis
The issue describes a Jinja slice error where:
- A SQL template contains multiple `{% set %}` statements following a `{% for %}` element
- When parsing this with sqlfluff, it produces a warning: "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: 286 != 58."
- The expected behavior is that Jinja should parse normally without warnings

### 1.2 Technical Details Highlighted
- **Error Message**: "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: 286 != 58"
- **Method/Component**: Jinja templating engine in sqlfluff
- **File Type**: `.sql` files with Jinja templates
- **Command**: `sqlfluff lint test.sql --dialect ansi`
- **Version**: sqlfluff 3.0.7, python 3.9

### 1.3 Clear Problem Statement
SQLFluff's Jinja templater is incorrectly calculating the length of the templated file when processing multiple consecutive `{% set %}` statements within a `{% for %}` loop. This causes a mismatch between the expected and actual slice lengths, resulting in a warning.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a SQL file with the problematic template:
   ```sql
   {% set max_partitions = 5 %}
   {% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
       SELECT * FROM my_table
       WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
   {% endfor %}
   ```
2. Run `sqlfluff lint test.sql --dialect ansi`
3. Observe the warning about length mismatch

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Need to understand how sqlfluff handles Jinja templating and slicing
- Should examine the templater code that calculates slice lengths
- Must ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality
- Should test with various combinations of Jinja constructs

     1	"""Defines the templaters."""
     2	
     3	import logging
     4	from bisect import bisect_left
     5	from typing import Any, Dict, Iterable, Iterator, List, NamedTuple, Optional, Tuple
     6	
     7	from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig
     8	from sqlfluff.core.errors import SQLFluffSkipFile, SQLTemplaterError
     9	from sqlfluff.core.helpers.slice import zero_slice
    10	
    11	# Instantiate the templater logger
    12	templater_logger = logging.getLogger("sqlfluff.templater")
    13	
    14	
    15	def iter_indices_of_newlines(raw_str: str) -> Iterator[int]:
    16	    """Find the indices of all newlines in a string."""
    17	    init_idx = -1
    18	    while True:
    19	        nl_pos = raw_str.find("\n", init_idx + 1)
    20	        if nl_pos >= 0:
    21	            yield nl_pos
    22	            init_idx = nl_pos
    23	        else:
    24	            break  # pragma: no cover TODO?
    25	
    26	
    27	def large_file_check(func):
    28	    """Raise an exception if the file is over a defined size.
    29	
    30	    Designed to be implemented as a decorator on `.process()` methods.
    31	
    32	    If no config is provided or the relevant config value is set
    33	    to zero then the check is skipped.
    34	    """
    35	
    36	    def _wrapped(
    37	        self, *, in_str: str, fname: str, config: Optional[FluffConfig] = None, **kwargs
    38	    ):
    39	        if config:
    40	            limit = config.get("large_file_skip_char_limit")
    41	            if limit:
    42	                templater_logger.warning(
    43	                    "The config value large_file_skip_char_limit was found set. "
    44	                    "This feature will be removed in a future release, please "
    45	                    "use the more efficient 'large_file_skip_byte_limit' instead."
    46	                )
    47	            if limit and len(in_str) > limit:
    48	                raise SQLFluffSkipFile(
    49	                    f"Length of file {fname!r} is over {limit} characters. "
    50	                    "Skipping to avoid parser lock. Users can increase this limit "
    51	                    "in their config by setting the 'large_file_skip_char_limit' "
    52	                    "value, or disable by setting it to zero."
    53	                )
    54	        return func(self, in_str=in_str, fname=fname, config=config, **kwargs)
    55	
    56	    return _wrapped
    57	
    58	
    59	class RawFileSlice(NamedTuple):
    60	    """A slice referring to a raw file."""
    61	
    62	    raw: str  # Source string
    63	    slice_type: str
    64	    source_idx: int  # Offset from beginning of source string
    65	    # Block index, incremented on start or end block tags, e.g. "if", "for".
    66	    # This is used in `BaseRule.discard_unsafe_fixes()` to reject any fixes
    67	    # which span multiple templated blocks.
    68	    block_idx: int = 0
    69	    # The command of a templated tag, e.g. "if", "for"
    70	    # This is used in template tracing as a kind of cache to identify the kind
    71	    # of template element this is without having to re-extract it each time.
    72	    tag: Optional[str] = None
    73	
    74	    def end_source_idx(self) -> int:
    75	        """Return the closing index of this slice."""
    76	        return self.source_idx + len(self.raw)
    77	
    78	    def source_slice(self) -> slice:
    79	        """Return a slice object for this slice."""
    80	        return slice(self.source_idx, self.end_source_idx())
    81	
    82	    def is_source_only_slice(self) -> bool:
    83	        """Based on its slice_type, does it only appear in the *source*?
    84	
    85	        There are some slice types which are automatically source only.
    86	        There are *also* some which are source only because they render
    87	        to an empty string.
    88	        """
    89	        # TODO: should any new logic go here?
    90	        return self.slice_type in ("comment", "block_end", "block_start", "block_mid")
    91	
    92	
    93	class TemplatedFileSlice(NamedTuple):
    94	    """A slice referring to a templated file."""
    95	
    96	    slice_type: str
    97	    source_slice: slice
    98	    templated_slice: slice
    99	
   100	
   101	class RawSliceBlockInfo(NamedTuple):
   102	    """Template-related info about the raw slices in a TemplateFile."""
   103	
   104	    # Given a raw file slace, return its block ID. Useful for identifying
   105	    # regions of a file with respect to template control structures (for, if).
   106	    block_ids: Dict[RawFileSlice, int]
   107	
   108	    # List of block IDs that have the following characteristics:
   109	    # - Loop body
   110	    # - Containing only literals (no templating)
   111	    literal_only_loops: List[int]
   112	
   113	
   114	class TemplatedFile:
   115	    """A templated SQL file.
   116	
   117	    This is the response of a templaters .process() method
   118	    and contains both references to the original file and also
   119	    the capability to split up that file when lexing.
   120	    """
   121	
   122	    def __init__(
   123	        self,
   124	        source_str: str,
   125	        fname: str,
   126	        templated_str: Optional[str] = None,
   127	        sliced_file: Optional[List[TemplatedFileSlice]] = None,
   128	        raw_sliced: Optional[List[RawFileSlice]] = None,
   129	    ):
   130	        """Initialise the TemplatedFile.
   131	
   132	        If no templated_str is provided then we assume that the file is NOT
   133	        templated and that the templated view is the same as the source view.
   134	
   135	        Args:
   136	            source_str (str): The source string.
   137	            fname (str): The file name.
   138	            templated_str (Optional[str], optional): The templated string.
   139	                Defaults to None.
   140	            sliced_file (Optional[List[TemplatedFileSlice]], optional): The sliced file.
   141	                Defaults to None.
   142	            raw_sliced (Optional[List[RawFileSlice]], optional): The raw sliced file.
   143	                Defaults to None.
   144	        """
   145	        self.source_str = source_str
   146	        # An empty string is still allowed as the templated string.
   147	        self.templated_str = source_str if templated_str is None else templated_str
   148	        # If no fname, we assume this is from a string or stdin.
   149	        self.fname = fname
   150	        # Assume that no sliced_file, means the file is not templated
   151	        self.sliced_file: List[TemplatedFileSlice]
   152	        if sliced_file is None:
   153	            if self.templated_str != self.source_str:  # pragma: no cover
   154	                raise ValueError("Cannot instantiate a templated file unsliced!")
   155	            # If we get here and we don't have sliced files,
   156	            # then it's raw, so create them.
   157	            self.sliced_file = [
   158	                TemplatedFileSlice(
   159	                    "literal", slice(0, len(source_str)), slice(0, len(source_str))
   160	                )
   161	            ]
   162	            assert (
   163	                raw_sliced is None
   164	            ), "Templated file was not sliced, but not has raw slices."
   165	            self.raw_sliced: List[RawFileSlice] = [
   166	                RawFileSlice(source_str, "literal", 0)
   167	            ]
   168	        else:
   169	            self.sliced_file = sliced_file
   170	            assert raw_sliced is not None, "Templated file was sliced, but not raw."
   171	            self.raw_sliced = raw_sliced
   172	
   173	        # Precalculate newlines, character positions.
   174	        self._source_newlines = list(iter_indices_of_newlines(self.source_str))
   175	        self._templated_newlines = list(iter_indices_of_newlines(self.templated_str))
   176	
   177	        # Consistency check raw string and slices.
   178	        pos = 0
   179	        rfs: RawFileSlice
   180	        for rfs in self.raw_sliced:
   181	            assert rfs.source_idx == pos, (
   182	                "TemplatedFile. Consistency fail on running source length"
   183	                f": {pos} != {rfs.source_idx}"
   184	            )
   185	            pos += len(rfs.raw)
   186	        assert pos == len(self.source_str), (
   187	            "TemplatedFile. Consistency fail on total source length"
   188	            f": {pos} != {len(self.source_str)}"
   189	        )
   190	
   191	        # Consistency check templated string and slices.
   192	        previous_slice = None
   193	        tfs: Optional[TemplatedFileSlice] = None
   194	        for tfs in self.sliced_file:
   195	            if previous_slice:
   196	                if tfs.templated_slice.start != previous_slice.templated_slice.stop:
   197	                    raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   198	                        "Templated slices found to be non-contiguous. "
   199	                        f"{tfs.templated_slice} (starting"
   200	                        f" {self.templated_str[tfs.templated_slice]!r})"
   201	                        f" does not follow {previous_slice.templated_slice} "
   202	                        "(starting "
   203	                        f"{self.templated_str[previous_slice.templated_slice]!r}"
   204	                        ")"
   205	                    )
   206	            else:
   207	                if tfs.templated_slice.start != 0:
   208	                    raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   209	                        "First Templated slice not started at index 0 "
   210	                        f"(found slice {tfs.templated_slice})"
   211	                    )
   212	            previous_slice = tfs
   213	        if self.sliced_file and templated_str is not None:
   214	            if tfs.templated_slice.stop != len(templated_str):
   215	                raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   216	                    "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: "
   217	                    f"{len(templated_str)} != {tfs.templated_slice.stop}."
   218	                )
   219	
   220	    @classmethod
   221	    def from_string(cls, raw: str) -> "TemplatedFile":
   222	        """Create TemplatedFile from a string."""
   223	        return cls(source_str=raw, fname="<string>")
   224	
   225	    def __repr__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   226	        """Return a string representation of the 'TemplatedFile' object."""
   227	        return "<TemplatedFile>"
   228	
   229	    def __str__(self) -> str:
   230	        """Return the templated file if coerced to string."""
   231	        return self.templated_str
   232	
   233	    def get_line_pos_of_char_pos(
   234	        self, char_pos: int, source: bool = True
   235	    ) -> Tuple[int, int]:
   236	        """Get the line number and position of a point in the source file.
   237	
   238	        Args:
   239	            char_pos: The character position in the relevant file.
   240	            source: Are we checking the source file (as opposed to the
   241	                templated file)
   242	
   243	        Returns:
   244	            line_number, line_position
   245	
   246	        """
   247	        if source:
   248	            ref_str = self._source_newlines
   249	        else:
   250	            ref_str = self._templated_newlines
   251	
   252	        nl_idx = bisect_left(ref_str, char_pos)
   253	
   254	        if nl_idx > 0:
   255	            return nl_idx + 1, char_pos - ref_str[nl_idx - 1]
   256	        else:
   257	            # NB: line_pos is char_pos+1 because character position is 0-indexed,
   258	            # but the line position is 1-indexed.
   259	            return 1, char_pos + 1
   260	
   261	    def _find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   262	        self,
   263	        templated_pos: int,
   264	        start_idx: Optional[int] = None,
   265	        inclusive: bool = True,
   266	    ) -> Tuple[int, int]:
   267	        """Find a subset of the sliced file which touch this point.
   268	
   269	        NB: the last_idx is exclusive, as the intent is to use this as a slice.
   270	        """
   271	        start_idx = start_idx or 0
   272	        first_idx = None
   273	        last_idx = start_idx
   274	        # Work through the sliced file, starting at the start_idx if given
   275	        # as an optimisation hint. The sliced_file is a list of TemplatedFileSlice
   276	        # which reference parts of the templated file and where they exist in the
   277	        # source.
   278	        for idx, elem in enumerate(self.sliced_file[start_idx:]):
   279	            last_idx = idx + start_idx
   280	            if elem[2].stop >= templated_pos:
   281	                if first_idx is None:
   282	                    first_idx = idx + start_idx
   283	                if elem[2].start > templated_pos:
   284	                    break
   285	                elif not inclusive and elem[2].start >= templated_pos:
   286	                    break
   287	        # If we got to the end add another index
   288	        else:
   289	            last_idx += 1
   290	        if first_idx is None:  # pragma: no cover
   291	            raise ValueError("Position Not Found")
   292	        return first_idx, last_idx
   293	
   294	    def raw_slices_spanning_source_slice(
   295	        self, source_slice: slice
   296	    ) -> List[RawFileSlice]:
   297	        """Return a list of the raw slices spanning a set of indices."""
   298	        # Special case: The source_slice is at the end of the file.
   299	        last_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[-1]
   300	        if source_slice.start >= last_raw_slice.source_idx + len(last_raw_slice.raw):
   301	            return []
   302	        # First find the start index
   303	        raw_slice_idx = 0
   304	        # Move the raw pointer forward to the start of this patch
   305	        while (
   306	            raw_slice_idx + 1 < len(self.raw_sliced)
   307	            and self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx + 1].source_idx <= source_slice.start
   308	        ):
   309	            raw_slice_idx += 1
   310	        # Find slice index of the end of this patch.
   311	        slice_span = 1
   312	        while (
   313	            raw_slice_idx + slice_span < len(self.raw_sliced)
   314	            and self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx + slice_span].source_idx
   315	            < source_slice.stop
   316	        ):
   317	            slice_span += 1
   318	        # Return the raw slices:
   319	        return self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx : raw_slice_idx + slice_span]
   320	
   321	    def templated_slice_to_source_slice(
   322	        self,
   323	        template_slice: slice,
   324	    ) -> slice:
   325	        """Convert a template slice to a source slice."""
   326	        if not self.sliced_file:
   327	            return template_slice  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   328	
   329	        ts_start_sf_start, ts_start_sf_stop = self._find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   330	            template_slice.start
   331	        )
   332	
   333	        ts_start_subsliced_file = self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:ts_start_sf_stop]
   334	
   335	        # Work out the insertion point
   336	        insertion_point = -1
   337	        for elem in ts_start_subsliced_file:
   338	            # Do slice starts and ends:
   339	            for slice_elem in ("start", "stop"):
   340	                if getattr(elem[2], slice_elem) == template_slice.start:
   341	                    # Store the lowest.
   342	                    point = getattr(elem[1], slice_elem)
   343	                    if insertion_point < 0 or point < insertion_point:
   344	                        insertion_point = point
   345	                    # We don't break here, because we might find ANOTHER
   346	                    # later which is actually earlier.
   347	
   348	        # Zero length slice.
   349	        if template_slice.start == template_slice.stop:
   350	            # Is it on a join?
   351	            if insertion_point >= 0:
   352	                return zero_slice(insertion_point)
   353	            # It's within a segment.
   354	            else:
   355	                if (
   356	                    ts_start_subsliced_file
   357	                    and ts_start_subsliced_file[0][0] == "literal"
   358	                ):
   359	                    offset = template_slice.start - ts_start_subsliced_file[0][2].start
   360	                    return zero_slice(
   361	                        ts_start_subsliced_file[0][1].start + offset,
   362	                    )
   363	                else:
   364	                    raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   365	                        "Attempting a single length slice within a templated section! "
   366	                        f"{template_slice} within {ts_start_subsliced_file}."
   367	                    )
   368	
   369	        # Otherwise it's a slice with length.
   370	
   371	        # Use a non inclusive match to get the end point.
   372	        ts_stop_sf_start, ts_stop_sf_stop = self._find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   373	            template_slice.stop, inclusive=False
   374	        )
   375	
   376	        # Update starting position based on insertion point:
   377	        if insertion_point >= 0:
   378	            for elem in self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:]:
   379	                if elem[1].start != insertion_point:
   380	                    ts_start_sf_start += 1
   381	                else:
   382	                    break
   383	
   384	        subslices = self.sliced_file[
   385	            # Very inclusive slice
   386	            min(ts_start_sf_start, ts_stop_sf_start) : max(
   387	                ts_start_sf_stop, ts_stop_sf_stop
   388	            )
   389	        ]
   390	        if ts_start_sf_start == ts_start_sf_stop:
   391	            if ts_start_sf_start > len(self.sliced_file):  # pragma: no cover
   392	                # We should never get here
   393	                raise ValueError("Starting position higher than sliced file position")
   394	            if ts_start_sf_start < len(self.sliced_file):  # pragma: no cover
   395	                return self.sliced_file[1].source_slice
   396	            else:
   397	                return self.sliced_file[-1].source_slice  # pragma: no cover
   398	        else:
   399	            start_slices = self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:ts_start_sf_stop]
   400	        if ts_stop_sf_start == ts_stop_sf_stop:  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   401	            stop_slic<response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you. You should retry this tool after you have searched inside the file with `grep -n` in order to find the line numbers of what you are looking for.</NOTE>

    50	class UndefinedRecorder:
    51	    """Similar to jinja2.StrictUndefined, but remembers, not fails."""
    52	
    53	    # Tell Jinja this object is safe to call and does not alter data.
    54	    # https://jinja.palletsprojects.com/en/2.9.x/sandbox/#jinja2.sandbox.SandboxedEnvironment.is_safe_callable
    55	    unsafe_callable = False
    56	    # https://jinja.palletsprojects.com/en/3.0.x/sandbox/#jinja2.sandbox.SandboxedEnvironment.is_safe_callable
    57	    alters_data = False
    58	
    59	    def __init__(self, name: str, undefined_set: set) -> None:
    60	        self.name = name
    61	        # Reference to undefined set to modify, it is assumed that the
    62	        # calling code keeps a reference to this variable to they can
    63	        # continue to access it after modification by this class.
    64	        self.undefined_set = undefined_set
    65	
    66	    def __str__(self) -> str:
    67	        """Treat undefined vars as empty, but remember for later."""
    68	        self.undefined_set.add(self.name)
    69	        return ""
    70	
    71	    def __getattr__(self, item) -> "UndefinedRecorder":
    72	        """Don't fail when called, remember instead."""
    73	        self.undefined_set.add(self.name)
    74	        return UndefinedRecorder(f"{self.name}.{item}", self.undefined_set)
    75	
    76	    def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs) -> "UndefinedRecorder":
    77	        """Don't fail when called unlike parent class."""
    78	        return UndefinedRecorder(f"{self.name}()", self.undefined_set)
    79	
    80	
    81	class JinjaTemplater(PythonTemplater):
    82	    """A templater using the jinja2 library.
    83	
    84	    See: https://jinja.palletsprojects.com/
    85	    """
    86	
    87	    name = "jinja"
    88	
    89	    class Libraries:
    90	        """Mock namespace for user-defined Jinja library."""
    91	
    92	        pass
    93	
    94	    @staticmethod
    95	    def _extract_macros_from_template(template, env, ctx):
    96	        """Take a template string and extract any macros from it.
    97	
    98	        Lovingly inspired by http://codyaray.com/2015/05/auto-load-jinja2-macros
    99	
   100	        Raises:
   101	            TemplateSyntaxError: If the macro we try to load has invalid
   102	                syntax. We assume that outer functions will catch this
   103	                exception and handle it appropriately.
   104	        """
   105	        from jinja2.runtime import Macro  # noqa
   106	
   107	        # Iterate through keys exported from the loaded template string
   108	        context = {}
   109	        # NOTE: `env.from_string()` will raise TemplateSyntaxError if `template`
   110	        # is invalid.
   111	        macro_template = env.from_string(template, globals=ctx)
   112	
   113	        # This is kind of low level and hacky but it works
   114	        try:
   115	            for k in macro_template.module.__dict__:
   116	                attr = getattr(macro_template.module, k)
   117	                # Is it a macro? If so install it at the name of the macro
   118	                if isinstance(attr, Macro):
   119	                    context[k] = attr
   120	        except UndefinedError:
   121	            # This occurs if any file in the macro path references an
   122	            # undefined Jinja variable. It's safe to ignore this. Any
   123	            # meaningful issues will surface later at linting time.
   124	            pass
   125	        # Return the context
   126	        return context
   127	
   128	    @classmethod
   129	    def _extract_macros_from_path(
   130	        cls,
   131	        path: List[str],
   132	        env: Environment,
   133	        ctx: Dict,
   134	        exclude_paths: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   135	    ) -> dict:
   136	        """Take a path and extract macros from it.
   137	
   138	        Args:
   139	            path (List[str]): A list of paths.
   140	            env (Environment): The environment object.
   141	            ctx (Dict): The context dictionary.
   142	            exclude_paths (Optional[[List][str]]): A list of paths to exclude
   143	
   144	        Returns:
   145	            dict: A dictionary containing the extracted macros.
   146	
   147	        Raises:
   148	            ValueError: If a path does not exist.
   149	            SQLTemplaterError: If there is an error in the Jinja macro file.
   150	        """
   151	        macro_ctx = {}
   152	        for path_entry in path:
   153	            # Does it exist? It should as this check was done on config load.
   154	            if not os.path.exists(path_entry):
   155	                raise ValueError(f"Path does not exist: {path_entry}")
   156	
   157	            if os.path.isfile(path_entry):
   158	                if exclude_paths:
   159	                    if cls._exclude_macros(
   160	                        macro_path=path_entry, exclude_macros_path=exclude_paths
   161	                    ):
   162	                        continue
   163	                # It's a file. Extract macros from it.
   164	                with open(path_entry) as opened_file:
   165	                    template = opened_file.read()
   166	                # Update the context with macros from the file.
   167	                try:
   168	                    macro_ctx.update(
   169	                        cls._extract_macros_from_template(template, env=env, ctx=ctx)
   170	                    )
   171	                except TemplateSyntaxError as err:
   172	                    raise SQLTemplaterError(
   173	                        f"Error in Jinja macro file {os.path.relpath(path_entry)}: "
   174	                        f"{err.message}",
   175	                        line_no=err.lineno,
   176	                        line_pos=1,
   177	                    ) from err
   178	            else:
   179	                # It's a directory. Iterate through files in it and extract from them.
   180	                for dirpath, _, files in os.walk(path_entry):
   181	                    for fname in files:
   182	                        if fname.endswith(".sql"):
   183	                            macro_ctx.update(
   184	                                cls._extract_macros_from_path(
   185	                                    [os.path.join(dirpath, fname)],
   186	                                    env=env,
   187	                                    ctx=ctx,
   188	                                    exclude_paths=exclude_paths,
   189	                                )
   190	                            )
   191	        return macro_ctx
   192	
   193	    def _extract_macros_from_config(self, config, env, ctx):
   194	        """Take a config and load any macros from it.
   195	
   196	        Args:
   197	            config: The config to extract macros from.
   198	            env: The environment.
   199	            ctx: The context.
   200	
   201	        Returns:
   202	            dict: A dictionary containing the extracted macros.
   203	        """
   204	        if config:
   205	            # This is now a nested section
   206	            loaded_context = (
   207	                config.get_section((self.templater_selector, self.name, "macros")) or {}
   208	            )
   209	        else:  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   210	            loaded_context = {}
   211	
   212	        # Iterate to load macros
   213	        macro_ctx = {}
   214	        for value in loaded_context.values():
   215	            try:
   216	                macro_ctx.update(
   217	                    self._extract_macros_from_template(value, env=env, ctx=ctx)
   218	                )
   219	            except TemplateSyntaxError as err:
   220	                raise SQLFluffUserError(
   221	                    f"Error loading user provided macro:\n`{value}`\n> {err}."
   222	                )
   223	        return macro_ctx
   224	
   225	    def _extract_libraries_from_config(self, config):
   226	        """Extracts libraries from the given configuration.
   227	
   228	        This function iterates over the modules in the library path and
   229	        imports them dynamically. The imported modules are then added to a 'Libraries'
   230	        object, which is returned as a dictionary excluding magic methods.
   231	
   232	        Args:
   233	            config: The configuration object.
   234	
   235	        Returns:
   236	            dict: A dictionary containing the extracted libraries.
   237	        """
   238	        # If a more global library_path is set, let that take precedence.
   239	        library_path = config.get("library_path") or config.get_section(
   240	            (self.templater_selector, self.name, "library_path")
   241	        )
   242	        if not library_path:
   243	            return {}
   244	
   245	        libraries = JinjaTemplater.Libraries()
   246	
   247	        # If library_path has __init__.py we parse it as one module, else we parse it
   248	        # a set of modules
   249	        is_library_module = os.path.exists(os.path.join(library_path, "__init__.py"))
   250	        library_module_name = os.path.basename(library_path)
   251	
   252	        # Need to go one level up to parse as a module correctly
   253	        walk_path = (
   254	            os.path.join(library_path, "..") if is_library_module else library_path
   255	        )
   256	
   257	        for module_finder, module_name, _ in pkgutil.walk_packages([walk_path]):
   258	            # skip other modules that can be near module_dir
   259	            if is_library_module and not module_name.startswith(library_module_name):
   260	                continue
   261	
   262	            # import_module is deprecated as of python 3.4. This follows roughly
   263	            # the guidance of the python docs:
   264	            # https://docs.python.org/3/library/importlib.html#approximating-importlib-import-module
   265	            spec = module_finder.find_spec(module_name)
   266	            module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
   267	            sys.modules[module_name] = module
   268	            spec.loader.exec_module(module)
   269	
   270	            if "." in module_name:  # nested modules have `.` in module_name
   271	                *module_path, last_module_name = module_name.split(".")
   272	                # find parent module recursively
   273	                parent_module = reduce(
   274	                    lambda res, path_part: getattr(res, path_part),
   275	                    module_path,
   276	                    libraries,
   277	                )
   278	
   279	                # set attribute on module object to make jinja working correctly
   280	                setattr(parent_module, last_module_name, module)
   281	            else:
   282	                # set attr on `libraries` obj to make it work in jinja nicely
   283	                setattr(libraries, module_name, module)
   284	
   285	        if is_library_module:
   286	            # when library is module we have one more root module in hierarchy and we
   287	            # remove it
   288	            libraries = getattr(libraries, library_module_name)
   289	
   290	        # remove magic methods from result
   291	        return {k: v for k, v in libraries.__dict__.items() if not k.startswith("__")}
   292	
   293	    @staticmethod
   294	    def _generate_dbt_builtins():
   295	        """Generate the dbt builtins which are injected in the context."""
   296	        # This feels a bit wrong defining these here, they should probably
   297	        # be configurable somewhere sensible. But for now they're not.
   298	        # TODO: Come up with a better solution.
   299	
   300	        class ThisEmulator:
   301	            """A class which emulates the `this` class from dbt."""
   302	
   303	            name = "this_model"
   304	            schema = "this_schema"
   305	            database = "this_database"
   306	
   307	            def __str__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   308	                return self.name
   309	
   310	        dbt_builtins = {
   311	            "ref": lambda *args: args[-1],
   312	            # In case of a cross project ref in dbt, model_ref is the second
   313	            # argument. Otherwise it is the only argument.
   314	            "source": lambda source_name, table: f"{source_name}_{table}",
   315	            "config": lambda **kwargs: "",
   316	            "var": lambda variable, default="": "item",
   317	            # `is_incremental()` renders as True, always in this case.
   318	            # TODO: This means we'll never parse other parts of the query,
   319	            # that are only reachable when `is_incremental()` returns False.
   320	            # We should try to find a solution to that. Perhaps forcing the file
   321	            # to be parsed TWICE if it uses this variable.
   322	            "is_incremental": lambda: True,
   323	            "this": ThisEmulator(),
   324	        }
   325	        return dbt_builtins
   326	
   327	    @classmethod
   328	    def _crawl_tree(cls, tree, variable_names, raw) -> Iterator[SQLTemplaterError]:
   329	        """Crawl the tree looking for occurrences of the undeclared values."""
   330	        # First iterate through children
   331	        for elem in tree.iter_child_nodes():
   332	            yield from cls._crawl_tree(elem, variable_names, raw)
   333	        # Then assess self
   334	        if (
   335	            isinstance(tree, jinja2.nodes.Name)
   336	            and getattr(tree, "name") in variable_names
   337	        ):
   338	            line_no: int = getattr(tree, "lineno")
   339	            tree_name: str = getattr(tree, "name")
   340	            line = raw.split("\n")[line_no - 1]
   341	            pos = line.index(tree_name) + 1
   342	            yield SQLTemplaterError(
   343	                f"Undefined jinja template variable: {tree_name!r}",
   344	                line_no=line_no,
   345	                line_pos=pos,
   346	            )
   347	
   348	    def _get_jinja_env(self, config=None):
   349	        """Get a properly configured jinja environment.
   350	
   351	        This method returns a properly configured jinja environment. It
   352	        first checks if the 'ignore' key is present in the config dictionary and
   353	        if it contains the value 'templating'. If so, it creates a subclass of
   354	        FileSystemLoader called SafeFileSystemLoader that overrides the
   355	        get_source method to handle missing templates when templating is ignored.
   356	        If 'ignore' is not present or does not contain 'templating', it uses the
   357	        regular FileSystemLoader. It then sets the extensions to ['jinja2.ext.do']
   358	        and adds the DBTTestExtension if the _apply_dbt_builtins method returns
   359	        True. Finally, it returns a SandboxedEnvironment object with the
   360	        specified settings.
   361	
   362	        Args:
   363	            config (dict, optional): A dictionary containing configuration settings.
   364	
   365	        Returns:
   366	            jinja2.Environment: A properly configured jinja environment.
   367	        """
   368	        macros_path = self._get_macros_path(config, "load_macros_from_path")
   369	        loader_search_path = self._get_loader_search_path(config)
   370	        final_search_path = (loader_search_path or []) + (macros_path or [])
   371	
   372	        ignore_templating = config and "templating" in config.get("ignore")
   373	        if ignore_templating:
   374	
   375	            class SafeFileSystemLoader(FileSystemLoader):
   376	                def get_source(self, environment, name, *args, **kwargs):
   377	                    try:
   378	                        if not isinstance(name, DummyUndefined):
   379	                            return super().get_source(
   380	                                environment, name, *args, **kwargs
   381	                            )
   382	                        raise TemplateNotFound(str(name))
   383	                    except TemplateNotFound:
   384	                        # When ignore=templating is set, treat missing files
   385	                        # or attempts to load an "Undefined" file as the first
   386	                        # 'base' part of the name / filename rather than failing.
   387	                        templater_logger.debug(
   388	                            "Providing dummy contents for Jinja macro file: %s", name
   389	                        )
   390	                        value = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(str(name)))[0]
   391	                        return value, f"{value}.sql", lambda: False
   392	
   393	            loader = SafeFileSystemLoader(final_search_path or [])
   394	        else:
   395	            loader = FileSystemLoader(final_search_path) if final_search_path else None
   396	        extensions = ["jinja2.ext.do"]
   397	        if self._apply_dbt_builtins(config):
   398	            extensions.append(DBTTestExtension)
   399	
   400	        return SandboxedEnvironment(
   401	            # We explicitly want to preserve newlines.
   402	            keep_trailing_n<response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you. You should retry this tool after you have searched inside the file with `grep -n` in order to find the line numbers of what you are looking for.</NOTE>

   769	    @staticmethod
   770	    def _rectify_templated_slices(
   771	        length_deltas: Dict[int, int], sliced_template: List[TemplatedFileSlice]
   772	    ):
   773	        """This method rectifies the source slices of a variant template.
   774	
   775	        :TRICKY: We want to yield variants that _look like_ they were
   776	        rendered from the original template. However, they were actually
   777	        rendered from a modified template, which means they have source
   778	        indices which won't line up with the source files. We correct that
   779	        here by using the length deltas generated earlier from the
   780	        modifications.
   781	
   782	        This should ensure that lint issues and fixes for the variants are
   783	        handled correctly and can be combined with those from the original
   784	        template.
   785	        """
   786	        # NOTE: We sort the stack because it's important that it's in order
   787	        # because we're going to be popping from one end of it. There's no
   788	        # guarantee that the items are in a particular order a) because it's
   789	        # a dict and b) because they may have been generated out of order.
   790	        delta_stack = sorted(length_deltas.items(), key=lambda t: t[0])
   791	
   792	        adjusted_slices: List[TemplatedFileSlice] = []
   793	        carried_delta = 0
   794	        for tfs in sliced_template:
   795	            if delta_stack:
   796	                idx, d = delta_stack[0]
   797	                if idx == tfs.source_slice.start + carried_delta:
   798	                    adjusted_slices.append(
   799	                        tfs._replace(
   800	                            # "stretch" the slice by adjusting the end more

    38	    unique_alternate_id: Optional[str]
    39	    alternate_code: Optional[str]
    40	    next_slice_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)
    41	    inside_block: bool = field(default=False)  # {% block %}
    42	
    43	
    44	class JinjaTracer:
    45	    """Records execution path of a Jinja template."""
    46	
    47	    def __init__(
    48	        self,
    49	        raw_str: str,
    50	        raw_sliced: List[RawFileSlice],
    51	        raw_slice_info: Dict[RawFileSlice, RawSliceInfo],
    52	        sliced_file: List[TemplatedFileSlice],
    53	        render_func: Callable[[str], str],
    54	    ):
    55	        # Input
    56	        self.raw_str = raw_str
    57	        self.raw_sliced = raw_sliced
    58	        self.raw_slice_info = raw_slice_info
    59	        self.sliced_file = sliced_file
    60	        self.render_func = render_func
    61	
    62	        # Internal bookkeeping
    63	        self.program_counter: int = 0
    64	        self.source_idx: int = 0
    65	
    66	    def trace(
    67	        self,
    68	        append_to_templated: str = "",
    69	    ) -> JinjaTrace:
    70	        """Executes raw_str. Returns template output and trace."""
    71	        trace_template_str = "".join(
    72	            (
    73	                cast(str, self.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code)
    74	                if self.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code is not None
    75	                else rs.raw
    76	            )
    77	            for rs in self.raw_sliced
    78	        )
    79	        trace_template_output = self.render_func(trace_template_str)
    80	        # Split output by section. Each section has two possible formats.
    81	        trace_entries: List[regex.Match[str]] = list(
    82	            regex.finditer(r"\0", trace_template_output)
    83	        )
    84	        # If the file has no templated entries, we should just iterate
    85	        # through the raw slices to add all the placeholders.
    86	        if not trace_entries:
    87	            for raw_idx, _ in enumerate(self.raw_sliced):
    88	                self.record_trace(0, raw_idx)
    89	
    90	        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
    91	            pos1 = match.span()[0]
    92	            try:
    93	                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
    94	            except IndexError:
    95	                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
    96	            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
    97	            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
    98	            if not m_id:
    99	                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   100	                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me continue reading this file to understand how the slicing works:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[100, 200]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py:
   100	                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
   101	                    "format."
   102	                )
   103	            if m_id.group(3):
   104	                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
   105	                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
   106	                # in raw_str.
   107	                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
   108	            else:
   109	                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
   110	                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
   111	                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])
   112	
   113	            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
   114	            target_inside_block = self.raw_slice_info[
   115	                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
   116	            ].inside_block
   117	            if not target_inside_block:
   118	                # Normal case: Walk through the template.
   119	                self.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, slice_length)
   120	            else:
   121	                # {% block %} executes code elsewhere in the template but does
   122	                # not move there. It's a bit like macro invocation.
   123	                self.record_trace(slice_length, target_slice_idx)
   124	
   125	        # TRICKY: The 'append_to_templated' parameter is only used by the dbt
   126	        # templater, passing "\n" for this parameter if we need to add one back.
   127	        # (The Jinja templater does not pass this parameter, so
   128	        # 'append_to_templated' gets the default value of "", empty string.)
   129	        # For more detail, see the comments near the call to slice_file() in
   130	        # plugins/sqlfluff-templater-dbt/sqlfluff_templater_dbt/templater.py.
   131	        templated_str = self.render_func(self.raw_str) + append_to_templated
   132	        return JinjaTrace(templated_str, self.raw_sliced, self.sliced_file)
   133	
   134	    def find_slice_index(self, slice_identifier: Union[int, str]) -> int:
   135	        """Given a slice identifier, return its index.
   136	
   137	        A slice identifier is a string like 00000000000000000000000000000002.
   138	        """
   139	        raw_slices_search_result = [
   140	            idx
   141	            for idx, rs in enumerate(self.raw_sliced)
   142	            if self.raw_slice_info[rs].unique_alternate_id == slice_identifier
   143	        ]
   144	        if len(raw_slices_search_result) != 1:
   145	            raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   146	                f"Internal error. Unable to locate slice for {slice_identifier}."
   147	            )
   148	        return raw_slices_search_result[0]
   149	
   150	    def move_to_slice(
   151	        self,
   152	        target_slice_idx: int,
   153	        target_slice_length: int,
   154	    ) -> Dict[int, List[int]]:
   155	        """Given a template location, walk execution to that point.
   156	
   157	        This updates the internal `program_counter` to the appropriate
   158	        location.
   159	
   160	        Returns:
   161	            :obj:`dict`: For each step in the template, a :obj:`list` of
   162	                which steps are accessible. In many cases each step will
   163	                only have one accessible next step (the following one),
   164	                however for branches in the program there may be more than
   165	                one.
   166	        """
   167	        step_candidates = {}
   168	        while self.program_counter < len(self.raw_sliced):
   169	            self.record_trace(
   170	                target_slice_length if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx else 0
   171	            )
   172	            current_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[self.program_counter]
   173	            if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx:
   174	                # Reached the target slice. Go to next location and stop.
   175	                self.program_counter += 1
   176	                break
   177	
   178	            # Choose the next step.
   179	            # We could simply go to the next slice (sequential execution).
   180	            candidates = [self.program_counter + 1]
   181	            # If we have other options, consider those.
   182	            candidates.extend(
   183	                filter(
   184	                    # They're a valid possibility if
   185	                    # they don't take us past the target.
   186	                    lambda idx: idx <= target_slice_idx,
   187	                    self.raw_slice_info[current_raw_slice].next_slice_indices,
   188	                )
   189	            )
   190	            # Choose the candidate that takes us closest to the target.
   191	            candidates.sort(key=lambda c: abs(target_slice_idx - c))
   192	            # Save all the candidates for each step so we can return them later.
   193	            step_candidates[self.program_counter] = candidates
   194	            # Step forward to the best step found.
   195	            self.program_counter = candidates[0]
   196	
   197	        # Return the candidates at each step.
   198	        return step_candidates
   199	
   200	    def record_trace(

   200	    def record_trace(
   201	        self,
   202	        target_slice_length: int,
   203	        slice_idx: Optional[int] = None,
   204	        slice_type: Optional[str] = None,
   205	    ) -> None:
   206	        """Add the specified (default: current) location to the trace.
   207	
   208	        Args:
   209	            target_slice_length (int): The length of the target slice.
   210	            slice_idx (Optional[int], optional): The index of the slice.
   211	            Defaults to None.
   212	            slice_type (Optional[str], optional): The type of the slice.
   213	            Defaults to None.
   214	        """
   215	        if slice_idx is None:
   216	            slice_idx = self.program_counter
   217	        if slice_type is None:
   218	            slice_type = self.raw_sliced[slice_idx].slice_type
   219	        self.sliced_file.append(
   220	            TemplatedFileSlice(

   222	                slice(

   224	                    (

   226	                        if slice_idx + 1 < len(self.raw_sliced)
   227	                        else len(self.raw_str)
   228	                    ),
   229	                ),
   230	                slice(self.source_idx, self.source_idx + target_slice_length),
   231	            )
   232	        )
   233	        if target_slice_length:
   234	            self.source_idx += target_slice_length

   240	
   241	    Attributes:
   242	        block_type (str): The block type that the Jinja tag maps to; eventually stored

   528	    def analyze(self, render_func: Callable[[str], str]) -> JinjaTracer:
   529	        """Slice template in jinja."""
   530	        # str_buff and str_parts are two ways we keep track of tokens received
   531	        # from Jinja. str_buff concatenates them together, while str_parts
   532	        # accumulates the individual strings. We generally prefer using
   533	        # str_parts. That's because Jinja doesn't just split on whitespace, so
   534	        # by keeping tokens as Jinja returns them, the code is more robust.
   535	        # Consider the following:
   536	        #   {% set col= "col1" %}
   537	        # Note there's no space after col. Jinja splits this up for us. If we
   538	        # simply concatenated the parts together and later split on whitespace,
   539	        # we'd need some ugly, fragile logic to handle various whitespace
   540	        # possibilities:
   541	        #   {% set col= "col1" %}
   542	        #   {% set col = "col1" %}
   543	        #   {% set col ="col1" %}
   544	        # By using str_parts and letting Jinja handle this, it just works.
   545	
   546	        str_buff = ""
   547	        str_parts = []
   548	
   549	        # https://jinja.palletsprojects.com/en/2.11.x/api/#jinja2.Environment.lex
   550	        block_idx = 0
   551	        for _, elem_type, raw in self.env.lex(self.raw_str):
   552	            if elem_type == "data":
   553	                self.track_literal(raw, block_idx)
   554	                continue
   555	            str_buff += raw
   556	            str_parts.append(raw)
   557	
   558	            if elem_type.endswith("_begin"):
   559	                self.handle_left_whitespace_stripping(raw, block_idx)
   560	
   561	            raw_slice_info: RawSliceInfo = self.make_raw_slice_info(None, None)
   562	            tag_contents = []
   563	            # raw_end and raw_begin behave a little differently in
   564	            # that the whole tag shows up in one go rather than getting
   565	            # parts of the tag at a time.
   566	            m_open = None
   567	            m_close = None
   568	            if elem_type.endswith("_end") or elem_type == "raw_begin":
   569	                block_type = self.block_types[elem_type]
   570	                block_tag = None
   571	                # Handle starts and ends of blocks
   572	                if block_type in ("block", "templated"):
   573	                    m_open = self.re_open_tag.search(str_parts[0])
   574	                    m_close = self.re_close_tag.search(str_parts[-1])
   575	                    if m_open and m_close:
   576	                        tag_contents = self.extract_tag_contents(
   577	                            str_parts, m_close, m_open, str_buff
   578	                        )
   579	
   580	                    if block_type == "block" and tag_contents:

   652	                        block_idx += 1
   653	                if block_type.startswith("block"):
   654	                    self.track_block_end(block_type, tag_contents[0])
   655	                    self.update_next_slice_indices(
   656	                        slice_idx, block_type, tag_contents[0]
   657	                    )
   658	                str_buff = ""
   659	                str_parts = []
   660	        return self._get_jinja_tracer(
   661	            self.raw_str,
   662	            self.raw_sliced,
   663	            self.raw_slice_info,
   664	            self.sliced_file,
   665	            render_func,
   666	        )
   667	
   668	    def track_templated(
   669	        self,
   670	        m_open: regex.Match[str],
   671	        m_close: regex.Match[str],
   672	        tag_contents: List[str],
   673	    ) -> RawSliceInfo:
   674	        """Compute tracking info for Jinja templated region, e.g. {{ foo }}.
   675	
   676	        Args:
   677	            m_open (regex.Match): A regex match object representing the opening tag.
   678	            m_close (regex.Match): A regex match object representing the closing tag.
   679	            tag_contents (List[str]): A list of strings representing the contents of the
   680	                tag.
   681	
   682	        Returns:
   683	            RawSliceInfo: A RawSliceInfo object containing the computed
   684	            tracking info.
   685	        """
   686	        unique_alternate_id = self.next_slice_id()
   687	        open_ = m_open.group(1)
   688	        close_ = m_close.group(1)
   689	        # Here, we still need to evaluate the original tag contents, e.g. in
   690	        # case it has intentional side effects, but also return a slice ID
   691	        # for tracking.
   692	        alternate_code = (
   693	            f"\0{unique_alternate_id} {open_} " f"{''.join(tag_contents)} {close_}"
   694	        )
   695	        return self.make_raw_slice_info(unique_alternate_id, alternate_code)
   696	
   697	    def track_call(
   698	        self,
   699	        m_open: regex.Match[str],
   700	        m_close: regex.Match[str],
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I need to understand the issue better. Let me create a debug script to see what's happening:

"""Detailed debug script to understand the slicing issue."""

import tempfile
import os
from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig

# Create the problematic SQL file
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}
"""

print("Testing the problematic SQL:")
print(repr(test_sql))
print("=" * 50)

# Create a temporary file
with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', suffix='.sql', delete=False) as f:
    f.write(test_sql)
    temp_filename = f.name

try:
    # Create a config
    config = FluffConfig.load_config_at_path()
    
    # Create templater
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    
    # Process the template
    templated_file, violations = templater.process(
        in_str=test_sql,
        fname=temp_filename,
        config=config
    )
    
    print(f"Source string length: {len(templated_file.source_str)}")
    print(f"Templated string length: {len(templated_file.templated_str)}")
    print(f"Number of sliced file elements: {len(templated_file.sliced_file)}")
    
    if templated_file.sliced_file:
        last_slice = templated_file.sliced_file[-1]
        print(f"Last slice templated stop: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
        print(f"Mismatch: {len(templated_file.templated_str)} != {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
        
        print("\nAll slices:")
        for i, slice_item in enumerate(templated_file.sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {slice_item}")
            
    print(f"\nTemplated string: {repr(templated_file.templated_str)}")
    
except Exception as e:
    print(f"ERROR: {e}")
    import traceback

# Create the problematic SQL file
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}
"""

print("=" * 50)

try:
    # Create a config
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Create templater
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    
    # Manually slice the file to see what happens
    from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
    env, live_context, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
        fname="test.sql", config=config
    )
    
    # Slice the file once rendered.
    raw_sliced, sliced_file, out_str = templater.slice_file(
        test_sql,
        render_func=render_func,
        config=config,
    )
    
    print(f"Source string length: {len(test_sql)}")
    print(f"Templated string length: {len(out_str)}")

    if sliced_file:
        last_slice = sliced_file[-1]
        print(f"Last slice templated stop: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
        print(f"Mismatch: {len(out_str)} != {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
        
        print("\nAll slices:")
        for i, slice_item in enumerate(sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {slice_item}")

    print("\nTrying to create TemplatedFile...")

        tf = TemplatedFile(
            source_str=test_sql,
            templated_str=out_str,
            fname="test.sql",
            sliced_file=sliced_file,
            raw_sliced=raw_sliced,
        )
        print("SUCCESS: TemplatedFile created without error")
    except SQLFluffSkipFile as e:
        print(f"ERROR: {e}")

Looking at the slice analysis:

1. The templated string is 287 characters long
2. But the sum of all templated slice lengths is only 58 characters
3. The difference is 229 characters

Looking at the templated string:
```
'\n\n    SELECT * FROM my_table\n    WHERE partition_key = 0;\n\n    SELECT * FROM my_table\n    WHERE partition_key = 1;\n\n    SELECT * FROM my_table\n    WHERE partition_key = 2;\n\n    SELECT * FROM my_table\n    WHERE partition_key = 3;\n\n    SELECT * FROM my_table\n    WHERE partition_key = 4;\n\n'
```

This makes sense - there are 5 iterations of the loop, each producing about 50+ characters.

But looking at the slices:
- Slice 0-7: These correspond to the initial {% set %} and {% for %} statements, but they all have templated_len=0 or 1 (just the newline)
- Slice 8: This is the big literal slice with templated_len=54, covering the main SQL content
- Slice 9: templated_len=1 for the {{ i }} variable
- Slice 10: templated_len=2 for some literal content  
- Slices 11-12: templated_len=0

The problem is that the Jinja tracer is not properly accounting for all the templated content that should be generated by the loop. It's only creating slices for one iteration of the loop, not for all 5 iterations.

The issue seems to be in how the Jinja tracer handles loops with multiple set statements. It's not generating the proper trace entries for all the iterations of the loop.

Let me look more closely at how the tracer works with loops.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the issue! The problem is that the Jinja tracer is not properly accounting for all the templated content generated by the loop. Looking at the slices, I can see that:

1. The templated string has 287 characters (5 iterations of the loop)
2. But the slices only account for 58 characters (apparently just 1 iteration)

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: State the Problem and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the problem clearly:

**Problem:** 
The Jinja tracer is not correctly handling loops with multiple consecutive {% set %} statements. While the trace template correctly captures all 5 iterations of the loop (showing 18 trace entries), the `tracer.trace()` method only creates slices for what appears to be a single iteration, leading to a mismatch between the actual templated string length (287) and what the slices account for (58).

**Location of the Problem:**
The issue is in `/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py`, specifically in the `trace()` method and how it processes the trace entries to build the sliced file.

**How the Test Reproduces the Issue:**
My debug scripts show that:
1. The trace template correctly generates output for all 5 iterations
2. There are 18 trace entries showing the repeating pattern
3. The final templated string is 287 characters (5 iterations × ~57 chars each)
4. But the sliced file only accounts for 58 characters (1 iteration)

**Best Practices for the Fix:**
1. The fix should be minimal and focused on the tracing logic
2. Should not break existing functionality
3. Should properly handle loops with multiple set statements
4. Should ensure slice continuity and correctness

**How to Fix the Problem:**
Looking at the trace entries, I can see that the same slice IDs are reused across iterations:
- ID `00000000000000000000000000000001` appears 5 times (once per iteration)
- ID `00000000000000000000000000000002` appears 5 times (once per iteration) 
- etc.

The issue is that the `record_trace` method is not properly accumulating the templated lengths for repeated slice IDs. When the same slice ID appears multiple times (due to loop iterations), the tracer should accumulate the lengths rather than just recording them once.

Looking at the `record_trace` method in tracer.py:
```python
def record_trace(
    self,
    target_slice_length: int,
    slice_idx: Optional[int] = None,
    slice_type: Optional[str] = None,
) -> None:
    # ...
    self.sliced_file.append(
        TemplatedFileSlice(
            slice_type,
            slice(
                self.raw_sliced[slice_idx].source_idx,
                (
                    self.raw_sliced[slice_idx + 1].source_idx
                    if slice_idx + 1 < len(self.raw_sliced)
                    else len(self.raw_str)
                ),
            ),
            slice(self.source_idx, self.source_idx + target_slice_length),
        )
    )
    if target_slice_length:
        self.source_idx += target_slice_length
```

The problem is that this method always appends a NEW slice, even when the same raw slice is processed multiple times due to a loop. Instead, for slices that are processed multiple times, it should either:
1. Extend the existing slice, or 
2. Create additional slices for the repeated processing

But looking more carefully at how TemplatedFile works, I think the real issue is conceptual. Each TemplatedFileSlice represents a mapping from a contiguous source region to a contiguous templated region. When a loop executes multiple times, the same source region maps to multiple different templated regions.

The fix should ensure that when a slice is processed multiple times due to looping, we create separate TemplatedFileSlice entries for each iteration, not just one.

   150	    def move_to_slice(
   151	        self,
   152	        target_slice_idx: int,
   153	        target_slice_length: int,
   154	    ) -> Dict[int, List[int]]:
   155	        """Given a template location, walk execution to that point.
   156	
   157	        This updates the internal `program_counter` to the appropriate
   158	        location.
   159	
   160	        Returns:
   161	            :obj:`dict`: For each step in the template, a :obj:`list` of
   162	                which steps are accessible. In many cases each step will
   163	                only have one accessible next step (the following one),
   164	                however for branches in the program there may be more than
   165	                one.
   166	        """
   167	        step_candidates = {}
   168	        while self.program_counter < len(self.raw_sliced):
   169	            self.record_trace(
   170	                target_slice_length if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx else 0
   171	            )
   172	            current_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[self.program_counter]
   173	            if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx:
   174	                # Reached the target slice. Go to next location and stop.
   175	                self.program_counter += 1
   176	                break
   177	
   178	            # Choose the next step.
   179	            # We could simply go to the next slice (sequential execution).
   180	            candidates = [self.program_counter + 1]
   181	            # If we have other options, consider those.
   182	            candidates.extend(
   183	                filter(
   184	                    # They're a valid possibility if
   185	                    # they don't take us past the target.
   186	                    lambda idx: idx <= target_slice_idx,
   187	                    self.raw_slice_info[current_raw_slice].next_slice_indices,
   188	                )
   189	            )
   190	            # Choose the candidate that takes us closest to the target.
   191	            candidates.sort(key=lambda c: abs(target_slice_idx - c))
   192	            # Save all the candidates for each step so we can return them later.
   193	            step_candidates[self.program_counter] = candidates
   194	            # Step forward to the best step found.
   195	            self.program_counter = candidates[0]
   196	
   197	        # Return the candidates at each step.
   198	        return step_candidates
   199	
   200	    def record_trace(
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I think I see the issue! The problem is subtle but important:

In the `move_to_slice` method, line 170:
```python
target_slice_length if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx else 0
```

This means that only when we're at the exact target slice do we record the actual `target_slice_length`. For all other slices we visit along the way, we record length 0.

But the issue is that for loops, the same slices get visited multiple times, and each time they should contribute their templated length, not just the first time.

"""Debug the flow of trace processing to understand the loop issue."""

import regex
from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core import FluffConfig
from sqlfluff.core.templaters.slicers.tracer import JinjaAnalyzer

# Create the problematic SQL file
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}
"""

print("Testing the problematic SQL:")
print("=" * 50)

try:
    # Create a config
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Create templater and get environment
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    env, live_context, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
        fname="test.sql", config=config
    )
    
    # Analyze the template
    analyzer = JinjaAnalyzer(test_sql, env)
    tracer = analyzer.analyze(render_func)
    
    # Let's manually simulate the trace processing to see what happens
    trace_template_str = "".join(
        (
            tracer.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code
            if tracer.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code is not None
            else rs.raw
        )
        for rs in tracer.raw_sliced
    )
    
    trace_template_output = render_func(trace_template_str)
    
    # Split output by section. Each section has two possible formats.
    trace_entries = list(regex.finditer(r"\0", trace_template_output))
    
    print("Processing trace entries:")
    tracer.sliced_file = []  # Reset sliced file
    tracer.program_counter = 0  # Reset program counter
    tracer.source_idx = 0  # Reset source index
    
    for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
        pos1 = match.span()[0]
        try:
            pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
        except IndexError:
            pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
        p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
        
        m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
        if m_id:
            if m_id.group(3):
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
            else:
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])

            target_slice_idx = tracer.find_slice_index(alt_id)
            print(f"Entry {match_idx}: ID={alt_id}, length={slice_length}, target_idx={target_slice_idx}")
            
            # Check if this is inside a block
            target_inside_block = tracer.raw_slice_info[
                tracer.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
            ].inside_block
            
            if not target_inside_block:
                print(f"  -> Normal case: moving to slice {target_slice_idx}")
                # This is where the issue might be!
                # Let's see what move_to_slice does
                old_pc = tracer.program_counter
                old_source_idx = tracer.source_idx
                old_sliced_len = len(tracer.sliced_file)
                
                tracer.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, slice_length)
                
                print(f"  -> PC: {old_pc} -> {tracer.program_counter}")
                print(f"  -> Source idx: {old_source_idx} -> {tracer.source_idx}")
                print(f"  -> Sliced file grew from {old_sliced_len} to {len(tracer.sliced_file)}")
                for i in range(old_sliced_len, len(tracer.sliced_file)):
                    print(f"    New slice {i}: {tracer.sliced_file[i]}")
            else:
                print(f"  -> Block case: recording trace directly")
                tracer.record_trace(slice_length, target_slice_idx)
        print()

    print(f"Final source index: {tracer.source_idx}")
    print(f"Number of slices: {len(tracer.sliced_file)}")

Looking at the debug output:
- After Entry 3, PC = 11 (past slices 8, 9, 10)
- For Entry 4 (same ID as Entry 1), PC = 11, target_idx = 8, but since PC > target_idx, `move_to_slice` doesn't do anything useful
- Same for Entries 5, 6, etc.

After Entry 3:
- PC = 11 
- For Entry 4: target_idx = 8, but PC (11) > target_idx (8), so the while loop `while self.program_counter < len(self.raw_sliced):` with `if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx:` will never trigger because PC is already past the target.

The real issue is that the tracer assumes a linear forward execution model, but loops require jumping back to earlier slices.
